### Page 2

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            2

ECHOES OF 2013 WITH FEW 
DIFFERENCES

Comparisons with 2013 are apt, though 
the backdrop differs.  As for the 
catalysts, markets reacted to the US 
Fed’s taper announcement in 2013, 
which pushed US yields higher  and led 
global investors to reduce exposure to 
vulnerable EM economies. As a result, 
capital inflows weakened, exposing 
funding mismatch es for countries, 
including India, that were running wide 
current account deficits.

In 2026, markets face the spectre of 
elevated oil prices, geopolitical 
uncertainty, global competition for 
capital, a firm dollar ( DXY appreciated 
+3.8% YTD) , and US inflation/ deficits 
which have settled at levels much higher 
than pre -pandemic years. This has 
pushed up UST yields, and the US Fed 
kicked off its rate-hike cycle in Sep26.

Against this backdrop, the RBI is 
pursuing the same objective , i.e., to 
ensure rupee stability, but the nature of 
the battle differs. The defence strategy 
has shifted from ‘crisis management’ 
mode in 2013 , due to weaker domestic 
macroeconomic balances (a wide current 
account gap, high inflation, a lower 
reserve buffer, and wide fiscal deficits) , 
to a ‘terms-of-trade shock and impact of 
a firm dollar’ in 2026.

Flashpoints are  nonetheless similar, 
with reliance on portfolio financing , 
leaving the external account vulnerable 
to shifts in global risk sentiment. 
Portfolio outflows have emerged as the 
primary transmission channel through 
which external shocks have translated 
into INR depreciation pressure , 
compounded by high energy prices and a 
firm dollar.

FX PLAYBOOK

While the response framework is similar 
between 2013 and this year, the broad 
sequence and nature of macroprudential 
measures differ. This year’s multi-
layered rupee defence toolkit has 
included:

-Direct FX defence  by way of 
intervention in the spot and forwards 
market. As of Aug26, the net forward 
dollar liabilities ballooned to $200bn, 
rising by ~$63bn vs . the month before . 
Recent sell/buy FX swaps are expected 
to lower this stock in the coming months.

-Attracting dollar inflows  by way of 
special swap facilities for FCNR deposits, 
and offshore borrowings , apart from 
liberalising taxes around debt inflows,

### Page 3

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            3

amongst others. We discussed this here, 
here, here and here.

Taking a proactive stance, a portion of 
existing FX reserves could be earmarked 
against forward liabilities (from maturing 
FCNR deposits) , helping to mitigate 
concerns that deposit maturities or debt 
repayments could trigger a sharp
increase in dollar ⁠demand down the line.

-Domestic liquidity management. Bonds, 
to some degree, are paying the price for 
the authorities' efforts to defend the 
rupee against the additional headwind of 
weak global conditions.  INR liquidity 
surged on the back of the swap windows, 
prompting the RBI to undertake open 
market operations, VRRR auctions, sell -
buy swaps, among others. While these 
have been effective in absorbing 
balances, bond yields have drifted higher. 
10Y yields are at three-year highs and are 
expected to climb further  (DBSf: 7.40 -
7.50% by year-end).

-Moderate FX demand and speculation . 
Earlier in the year, the RBI had 
introduced change s to the banks’ net 
open position framework, and thereafter 
partially rolled back a few curbs. 
Prudential measures on FX exposures 
and hedging -related measures were 
announced in Oct26, which include d the 
introduction of a daily cash reserve with 
the RBI equal to 20% of the rupee 
equivalent of the notional value for INR -
linked derivative contracts exceeding 
$2mn used to hedge current account 
transactions, referred to as the Foreign 
Exchange Risk Reserve (FERR) . 
Subsequent clarifications provided 
operational details on where the FERR 
would be maintained and that it would be 
subject to audits.

Since 12 Oct, the RBI has been providing 
dollars via designated banks to the three 
state-owned oil -marketing companies 
(Indian Oil, BPCL, and HPCL) to meet the 
their daily dollar requirements, thereby 
diverting related spot-market demand.

On hedging-related matters, the limit for 
undertaking foreign exchange derivative 
transactions without proving an 
underlying commercial exposure was 
cut to $5mn from $100mn across all ADs 
and exchange -traded currency 
derivatives. Restrictions on Rebooking , 
i.e., a uthorized dealers are prohibited 
from allowing customers to rebook 
cancelled rupee -linked derivative 
contracts (though rollovers upon 
maturity remain permitted). Lastly, 
banks must obtain written confirmation 
from users that the same underlying 
exposure has not been hedged with 
another dealer.  Data from CCI , cited by

### Page 4

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            4

Bloomberg, pointed to importers’ 
forward dollar purchases rising 44% on 
the year to a monthly average of $64bn.

-Current account management . Efforts 
to contain structural FX outflows by way 
of higher duties and taxes on gold 
imports discourage non -essential 
purchases and production incentives to 
improve export competitiveness.

IMPACT

To assess whether the playbook has 
worked, we observe:

a) Rupee has enjoyed a brief respite. 
Despite oil prices at $105/bbl at the time 
of writing and elevated global yields, 
USDINR fell from near 96.80 to wards 
95.00, owing to measures announced in 
Oct26 following the central bank’s 25bp 
hike. Beyond temporary relief, global 
catalysts are likely to be back to the fore 
and weigh on the currency (see chart);

b) Sharp inflows under the swap 
facilities pushed the foreign reserves 
stock to a record high, providing 
policymakers sufficient buffer to 
navigate future shocks from a position of 
strength. A strong interventionist 
response has since led to a softening in 
the overall stock (see chart).

c) Macroprudential measures have led to 
distortions in other parts of the market . 
For instance, strong swap -related 
inflows had inorganically pushed 
onshore INR liquidity up sharply, 
necessitating absorption measures. 
Similarly, NOP restrictions and the more 
recent tighter requirements on hedging 
activity and counterparty transactions  
had some after -effects. Forward points 
have surged this week , with financial 
institutions pointing to relative illiquidity 
in the currency forwards market  on 
volumes and activity basis, and fewer 
avenues to deal with dollar supply.

### Page 5

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            5

d) Swap-related windows are a timely 
support for the country’s balance of 
payments position. From expectations of 
a third consecutive year of BOP deficit in 
FY27, the math has now swung to a 
potential surplus, even as current 
account worries remain. With energy 
prices settling north of $100/bbl, 
keeping the monthly trade deficit near 
$30bn, our FY27 current account deficit 
forecast of 1.1% of GDP faces  upside 
risks of around 0.3-0.5% of GDP. We note 
the s harp jump in ECB outflows in S ep, 
though see that as a temporary overshot 
of leverage-FCNR activity.

WHAT LIES AHEAD

Authorities are likely not done yet and 
remain ready to take further action, as 
required. RBI Governor Malhotra 
reiterated the authorities’ serious intent 
of stabilising the currency in a recent 
interview (Moneycontrol, 13 Oct), 
emphasising that “The RBI is determined 
to do whatever is necessary to ensure 
orderly movement in the foreign 
exchange market,”. He also highlighted 
that recent steps were not tantamount to 
capital control measures that were 
aimed at ensuring that forex markets 
serve genuine hedging needs rather than 
speculative bets.  Market participants 
opine that FX volumes and interest are 
likely to be subdued in a few pockets on 
the back of the recent measures, 
consequently stabilizing the rupee.

Unlike in 2013, when policy tightening 
was a part of the initial response, the 
2026 strategy has prioritised targeted 
FX and regulatory measures.  The 
sequencing suggests a deliberate effort 
to distinguish actions aimed at 
preserving currency stability from those 
intended to achieve domestic monetary 
objectives, with rates remaining a 
potential, though not immediate, tool in 
the defence framework.

However, global developments have 
altered that dynamic . Rising US yields 
and a gradual increase in the US Fed 
Funds rate (DBS baseline: a total of 75bp 
hikes) have compressed IN -US spreads. 
The recent sell -off in INR benchmark

### Page 6

India: Degrees of rupee defence October 16, 2026 
                                                                                                                                            6

bonds has effectively widened Gsec -
UST spreads across maturities, in 
contrast to the compression witnessed in 
regional high-yielders.

The latest announcements may not 
fully quell speculation over further 
policy action.

Upcoming measures might include -

-Markets anticipate relief on the long -
term capital gains tax structure on 
equities to draw in longer-duration 
inflows. This measure will boost inflows 
if it coincides with other factors that 
make domestic equities attractive for 
thematic investing , as global risk -free 
rates moderate.

-Street expectations also include steps 
to boost inward dollar repatriation by 
exporters to boost domestic dollar 
supply.

-Wider policy rate corridor akin to 2013.

-Policy rate response. While this might 
not be a panacea, it is a likely necessary 
condition. Raising the neutral rate might 
have mixed success in attracting capital 
market inflows ; though rate-sensitive 
flows are likely to get a fillip as investors 
seek attractive rate differentials in the 
region.

An adequate real rate cushion would 
also align with the evolving domestic 
inflation realities, which are expected to 
accelerate to 6% in the Oct-Dec quarter 
and remain above 5% well into 2027.

Given fluid global developments, we 
outline three scenarios on the rate path 
ahead. Our baseline assumption  is for 
75bp hikes in this cycle  (including Oct), 
spread between the Dece mber and 
February 2027 meetings. We don’t rule 
out an intermeeting rate hike before the 
December meeting, contingent on 
rupee movements.

Under the second scenario , s hould 
depreciation pressures on the rupee 
return, the 50bp hike might be brought 
forward to December , with another in 
February, taking cumulative hikes in this 
cycle to 100bp. The t hird scenario  is a 
more aggressive 150bp worth of hikes in 
this cycle, with 50bp front -loaded in 
December, and the rest spilling into 2027. 
Factors that  will determine whether 
these scenarios materialise are the 
resumption and scale of rupee 
depreciation, oil price direction, US Fed 
Funds rate increases (DBSf: total 75bps) 
and other risk-off events.

Path ahead

Despite these measures, we see limited 
room for sustained INR appreciation on 
grounds of manufacturing/ trade 
competitiveness. A challenging flows 
portfolio flows outlook leaves the d oor 
open to further gradual rupee 
depreciation subject to global 
developments, with high sensitivity to oil 
price movements and risk sentiments . A 
strong rate response will help introduce  
favourable rate differentials and reduce 
currency volatility.

Style Exposure
We are incrementally positive on Jentech with multiple catalysts ahead. Near-term,

we believe the company’s 3Q26 earnings could be the first driver with a potential
GM beat, which should be reported in the second week of Nov. Longer-term, we
believe the drivers are from the incremental adoption of the removable lid in some
Nvidia Rubin GPUs in 2027 (in addition to Rubin Ultra), followed by the
meaningful Google TPU v9 spec upgrade potential in 2028 as another multi-fold
content increase story (based on JPMe), along with a structural margin expansion

trend given continued GPU and ASIC upgrades. Thus, we revise up our 2027E and
2028E EPS materially and are now 28% and 18% above the current BBGe
consensus.
• 3Q26 preview: expecting a stronger GM growth; 4Q26 outlook: slightly
better rev growth. We revise up our 3Q26 GM to 50.1% (vs current BBGe
consensus at 48.7%), which implies another quarter of strong GM expansion
(vs 2Q/1Q26 GM at 46.8%/41.8%), driven by better product mix to more AI
server business. Reported 3Q26 revenue of NT$9.5bn was largely in line with

the company’s guidance, and the 30% QoQ growth was mainly driven by the
decent ramp of Rubin GPU. As the Rubin 1-piece lid shipment should remain
at high volume into 4Q26, we believe the company’s revenue momentum will
also continue, with incremental growth from small volume of removable lid
shipment. Hence, we are now modeling Jentech’s 4Q26 revenue to grow 3%
QoQ, with GM further expanding thanks to ongoing mix enhancement.
• Further 2027 upside from incremental Rubin removable lid adoption;

2028 another earnings level-up year driven by meaningful upgrade in
TPU v9.We revise up our 2027E and 2028E revenue estimates materially by
22% and 14%, which are now 24% and 16% above consensus, respectively. For
the 2027 upward revision, it mainly reflects the incremental adoption of
removable lid (3-piece lid) in partial Rubin GPU from the next 1-2 quarter, in
addition to Rubin Ultra, as long as the company can ramp up its yield and
production of removable lid in the next few months with on-schedule capacity

expansion (new Taoyuan Aerotropolis plant) and equipment move-in (e.g. the
in-house screw locking equipment). More encouragingly, we believe 2028 will
see even greater earnings upside with Google TPU v9 likely upgrading its heat
spreader solution significantly to 2-piece or 3-piece lid design, in our view, as
the potential chip size (4-die) and TDP (potentially 2k+W or even higher)
should be a big level-up compared to v8, which makes the current stiffener-

only solution insufficient. As the content of 2-piece or 3-piece lid solution
could be 5-10x higher than the v8 stiffener, Google TPU v9 (with meaningful
volume) could drive the next wave of the earnings upgrade cycle for Jentech
from 2028 (following Nvidia Rubin/Rubin Ultra as the major driver in 2027).
• Implications. We lift our 2027E and 2028E EPS by 28% and 17% to NT$193
and NT$295, respectively, reflecting the incremental removable lid adoption

Summary Investment Thesis and Valuation
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component
maker worldwide, with leading production capabilities across
heat spreader products (including stiffener and all kinds of lids).
We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-
28, driven by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI

chips and increasing TAM into new solutions (e.g., CPO switch
ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server
sockets for chip customers. We believe Jentech’s technology
leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings
power in the long term.
Valuation
Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which

is largely in line with Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the
AI server boom started.

Investment Thesis, Valuation and Risks
Jentech Precision Industrial Co. (Overweight; Price Target: NT
$8,800.00)
Investment Thesis
Jentech (OW) is the largest packaging-level cooling component maker worldwide, with
leading production capabilities across heat spreader products (including stiffener and all
kinds of lids). We expect the company to show a ~90% EPS CAGR over 2024-28, driven

by solid heat spreader content growth in future-gen AI chips and increasing TAM into new
solutions (e.g. CPO switch ASIC). In addition, the company is reinforcing its product
portfolio with new offerings in cold plate and CPU server sockets for chip customers. We
believe Jentech’s technology leadership in the structural cooling upgrade trend and its new
TAM expansion will continue to support its superior earnings power in the long term.
Valuation

Our Dec-27 PT of NT$8,800 is based on 30x 2028E P/E, which is largely in line with
Jentech’s three-year average P/E when the AI server boom started.
Risks to Rating and Price Target
Downside risks to our rating and price target include: 1) Potential delay in mainstream 3-
piece/removable lid design adoption, and 2) market share loss from competition.

近三月(%)
► 美股表現相對強勁 ，主要受科技 與半導體上
漲帶動 。其中標普 500與那斯達克指數一度
創高，費半指數漲勢則更為顯著。反映9月非
農數據放緩推升 10月聯準會按兵不動預期所
帶來的激勵效果延續 。另一方面 ，歐股則是
不漲反跌 ，因傳出法國債務風險疑慮與政局
不穩等 消息，帶動法國股市下跌 。日股在半
導體與金融聯袂反彈下，推動指數一度收復7

萬點整數關卡 ，港股則在陸股國慶假期休市
量能縮減下，指數亦表現疲弱。
► 產業 方面 ，馬斯 克 稱正與 台 積電 討 論 參與
Terafab 德州晶片計畫 ，加上超微表示將於
2027年大幅增加晶片供應 ，以因應AI產品需
求快速成長 ，執行長蘇姿 丰更赴 台與供應鏈
夥伴討論擴充 CPU與GPU產能，顯示AI半導
體長期發展趨勢強勁 ，激勵科技相關類股表
現。此外，G7釋放緊急原油與柴油庫存後 ，
減輕市場對能源價格與通膨的部分 擔憂，亦
帶動非必需消費產業上漲。

近三月(%)
► 美國9月非農數據公布後一度令 10年期公債
殖利率出現明顯回落 。然觀察最新 ISM服務
業之價 格分 項 指 數 較上月 出現反彈 ， 並創
2022年7月以來 最高水準 ，反映運輸 及原物
料成本上升 ，顯示通膨壓力仍可能持續向其
他產業傳導 ，公債殖利率因而再度反彈並持
續於高檔徘迴 ，多數債券市場在利率高檔震
盪環境下亦呈現跌多漲少現象。
► 受法國債務問題與西班牙提前大選等歐洲 政
治與經濟消息影響 ，歐元走勢疲弱 。儘管近
期日圓於日銀9月升息後不升反貶，然市場亦
擔憂持續走貶可能再度引發干預 ，令短線日
圓呈現區間震盪走勢。
► G7宣布透過國際能源總署協調釋放 1億桶柴
油及原油等緊急 儲備，令油價短線回落 ，然
隨中東衝突前景未明 ，供應不穩之變數短期
難解，多空因素交錯恐使油價於高檔進行反
覆波動。

► 美國經濟仍具韌性 ，惟通膨壓力猶存 。觀察
美國9月ISM服務業指數表現 ，內需活動維持
穩健，企業投入成本壓力亦有所升溫；另一
方面，受地緣政治因素影響 ，美國柴油價格
大幅攀升 ，而柴油為卡車運輸 、農業及製造
業的重要燃料 ，若價格持續維持高檔 ，恐進
一步增添通膨壓力 。考量聯準會偏鷹立場 ，
且當前通膨降溫進程不如預期 ，年底前仍保
有升息空間。
► 美國債務總額突破40兆美元，預估2026年公
眾持有聯邦債務占 GDP的比率將達 101%，
財政可持續性風險升溫 。與此同時 ，AI基礎

建設舉債熱潮 ，疊加中東局勢動盪帶來的通
膨不確定性 ，迫使投資人重新定價長天期債
券風險 ，要求更高的風險溢酬 。隨實質殖利
率上行，美債10年期殖利率揚升並持續高懸
於5%水準之上，30年期長債殖利率亦同步站
上近20年來高點 。隨長天期債券利率續揚 ，
殖利率曲線趨陡峭 ，債券配置宜先避開長天
期部位，聚焦中短天期債券鎖利機會。
熱門議題
熱門議題
柴油價格升至歷史高位，殖利率曲線呈現上揚通膨降溫進展不如預期，未來仍存升息空間
債券利率
年底升息預期不減，債券聚焦中短天期鎖利機會

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 距離美國期中選舉投票僅剩不到1個月，觀察
Polymarket賭盤預估數據顯示 ，眾議院選情
較為明朗，民主黨有超過9成的機率控制眾議
院。但參議院選情則尚有變數 ，今年以來兩
黨均互有領先 ，雖然數據顯示民主黨掌控參
議院機率呈現上揚 ，然隨時序進入選前最後
衝刺期 ，參議院能否亦由民主黨掌控仍待觀
察。故就選前而言 ，由於不同政黨的政策傾
向差異 ，潛在受惠產業亦有不同 ，選舉結果
的不確定性恐令投資人不易大舉押注股市。
► 另一方面 ，油價高檔徘迴 ，導致年底前通 膨
擔憂仍存 ，加上AI企業發債需求成長 、美國
財政體質惡化等 因素共同發酵 ，推動近期 美
債殖利率逐步攀高 ，並令股市預期本益 比壓
力延續 ，進而限縮了投資人短期對美股的追
價動能，短期指數恐維持震盪輪動格局 。可
留意抗波動 、體質佳且具價值優勢之標的 ，
待期中選舉結束與3Q26財報展開後，不確定
性減退將有助資金回歸長線AI題材。
殖利率攀升令美股本益比面臨壓力參議院期中選舉選情仍具變數
期中選前擾動與殖利率走高，美股短期恐仍震盪輪動

### Page 7

7
投資焦點日股
► 9月日本銀行決議升息 1碼，明確表示潛在
CPI已接近2%，未來也將繼續調高利率 ，政
策重點開始轉向防範通膨向上偏離目標 。然
因有2位委員反對升息，加上總裁植田和男會
後談話偏向中性 ，令市場解讀此次升息未如
預期鷹派，美日利差不易快速縮窄，故9月會
議後日元反而呈現利多出盡的回貶反應。
► 觀察今年以來日股面臨短線日圓升值的狀態
並未出現顯著走弱 態勢。一方面反映企業治
理改革推動股東報酬 提升已成為 支撐股價的
重要結構性因素 。另一方面 ，升息代表日本
經濟已具備一定的健康程度 ，貨幣政策正常
化將不致對需求帶來衝擊 。因此 ，隨BoJ延
續升息方向 ，將擴大銀行淨利差 ，改善其獲
利結構，並提升金融資產收益 。儘管近期整
體日股預期獲利成長走勢略顯停滯 ，然日本
銀行股 在總 體經 濟 與貨幣 政策環 境的 支持
下，獲利成長有望逆勢攀升 ，料將成為後續
資金關注重點。
日圓升值對日股影響似乎有限，關注銀行股獲利走強BoJ升息方向延續，惟其步伐仍取決於經濟與物價

BoJ政策保持正常化路線，日本銀行股有望持續受惠

### Page 8

8
股市
 公債殖利率走高雖可能壓抑 股債市場近期 動能，然而觀察美股第三季獲利年增 率有望仍為雙位數成
長，或支撐股市表現 。市場持續關注AI資本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場
動盪，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評
價面壓力等紛擾 帶來干擾，可布局防禦 股增強抗震力 。長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且
基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融及醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨 發展。殖利率偏高環境建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走
升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸

引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
 中東地緣衝突短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期抗波動，逢回可分批布局仍具長線趨勢之AI與科技產業

### Page 15

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 1

From:    KP - FOMO 研究院  <fomosoc@substack.com>
Subject: Palantir 新簽訂單放緩了？蘋果服務被 AI 影響了？財報焦點 : Palantir + Apple 蘋果 | 26 年 10 ⽉
Date:    10 hours ago
Palantir 新簽訂單放緩了？蘋果服務被 AI 影響了？財報焦點 : Palantir + Apple 蘋果 | 26 年 10 ⽉
View in browser
Palantir 新簽訂單放緩了？蘋果服務
被 AI 影響了？財報焦點 : Palantir +
Apple 蘋果 | 26 年 10 ⽉
KP@FOMOSOC AND BENITA HSU
NOV 3 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP
這次我們想做個新嘗試。
之前幾期的「財報解剖」⼀向以極致的深度著稱，但⽼實說，並⾮每次財報公
佈，都有必要把所有維度拉出來重新解構⼀遍。
有些時候，最關鍵的變化其實只集中在兩三個核⼼變數上。
因此，我們推出了這個更聚焦、節奏更快的變體版。

雖然篇幅縮減，但我們對商業本質的思考沒有打折。這裡保留了最精華的洞
察，保證依然⽐市⾯上絕⼤多數的財報分析更有⾒解。
這份分析由我們的分析師  Benita 主導撰寫，我負責校正觀點並做最後把關，希
望能⽤更⾼效的⽅式，幫⼤家抓出最值得關注的產業訊號。
Palantir ⼜⼀次完美財報，企業 AI
開始真正變現，但新簽訂單卻放緩了？

### Page 2

Palantir 第三季⼜交出了⼀份近乎沒有瑕疵的財報。
2026 年第三季營收來到  23.13 億美元，年增  96% 、季增  20% ，創下公司史上
最⾼的年增率，同時也是連續第  13 個季度營收成⻑加速。

### Page 3

其中美國商業營收⼤增  148% ，調整後營業利益率從去年同期的  51% 躍升⾄
62% ，推升  Rule of 40 達到  158% 。
同時，公司還把第四季營收指引調⾼到約  26.51 億美元，⾼於市場原本預期的
約  24.7 億美元（⾼出約  7% ）。
只要第四季順利達成指引， Palantir 的年化營收規模就將正式跨過  100 億美元
⼤關。
不過， Palantir 的「好」，已經不是新聞了。
我們會將這次的分析分為 4 個重點。
重點⼀：企業 AI 開始真正變現 —— 從百萬美元試點，⾛向千萬美
元的營運系統
過去這兩年，許多企業在導⼊ AI 時，最常⾒的做法就是去買⼤模型的 API 、做
個內部聊天機器⼈，或是花錢做⼀堆概念驗證（ PoC ）。
但試了⼀圈之後，許多企業開始感到挫折。
⼀⽅⾯，對外串接純⼤模型供應商，很難完全保護公司內部的機密資料與營運
知識；另⼀⽅⾯，讓 AI 寫寫信、做個摘要並不難，真正的挑戰在於如何讓 AI
融⼊公司的⽇常⼯作流程，並創造出⾜以回本的經濟效益。

為了解決這個痛點， Palantir 過去透過實戰⼯作坊（ AIP Bootcamp ，近期已進⼀
步演進為打造 AI 代理的 AgentCamp ），讓企業帶著⾃⼰的真實資料，在短時間
內做出⼀套能實際運作的系統。
到了今年，這套模式更延伸到「主權 AI （ Sovereign AI ）」。
這裡談的 AI 主權，重點在於讓企業⾃⼰掌握資料、業務邏輯與模型選擇權。 企
業可以⾃由更換底層模型，並把真正值錢的營運知識留在⾃⼰的系統裡。
在這個架構下，企業可以利⽤⾃⼰累積的營運資料，在 Palantir 的 Ontology （本
體論）架構下評估並微調開放權重模型，完全不必每件事都去呼叫最昂貴的頂
級⼤模型。
在這次法說會上，有兩個案例特別有說服⼒：
第⼀個是國防科技公司 L3Harris 。

### Page 4

他們利⽤⾃家資料微調開放模型後，在不到 48 ⼩時內，就讓這套主權模型的表
現超越了原本使⽤的前沿⼤模型，⽽且初步結果顯⽰整體成本⼤幅降低了
95% 。
第⼆個案例則是連 NVIDIA ⾃⼰都在⽤。
NVIDIA 透過 Palantir 評估並重新訓練（ post-training ）⾃家的開放模型，結果成
功讓旗下體積最⼩、效率最⾼的模型，在表現上⼤幅超越了⾃家最⼤的模型。
企業在⽇常營運中，往往更需要⼀個真正懂內部業務、且成本合理的專屬
模型，勝過⼀味追求最昂貴卻不懂公司流程的通⽤⼤模型。
舉個簡單的例⼦，⼀家製造商如果要⽤ AI 判斷哪些零件該優先採購，它真正需
要的是模型能讀懂公司的供應商名單、⽣產排程、庫存限制和交貨承諾，並根
據最新狀況隨時調整。
Palantir 想掌握的，正是把底層模型轉化為實際⼯作流程的這⼀層價值。
當企業發現這套做法真的能解決問題，從試點轉為正式⼤單的速度就相當快。

### Page 5

這次財報中， Palantir 公布了四個在⼀兩季內從⼩型試點迅速擴張成⼤型合約的
案例，擴張後的年化合約價值（ ACV ）合計達到 7,650 萬美元：
家電製造商： 從 100 萬美元的初始試點，擴張⾄ 1,500 萬美元的年化合
約。
⼯業材料公司： 從近 300 萬美元試點，擴張⾄ 2,900 萬美元。
全球運動品牌： 從 160 萬美元試點，擴張⾄約 2,100 萬美元。
服飾企業： 透過 AgentCamp 啟動 250 萬美元試點，隨後擴張⾄ 1,150 萬美
元。
其中，那家家電製造商的變化特別有代表性。
他們在第⼆季才剛花 100 萬美元試⽔溫，很快就轉成 300 萬美元的合約；結果
到了第三季，合約⾦額⼜⼀⼝氣放⼤五倍來到 1,500 萬美元，使⽤範圍直接橫
跨銷售（商務）、財務與供應鏈三⼤部⾨。

### Page 6

這代表客戶⼀旦把單⼀功能跑順了，就會開始把不同部⾨的⼯作流程全部串進
同⼀個平台，預算規模也跟著翻倍跳升。
重點⼆：美國商業營收⾸度超⾞政府 —— ⽼客戶越買越多，撐起
第⼆顆成⻑引擎
當單⼀客戶的合約⾦額從⼀百萬變成上千萬美元，反映在全公司的總體財報
上，就促成了這次最具指標性的轉折：
Palantir 的美國商業營收，歷史上第⼀次超越了美國政府營收。
我們可以看看第三季美國市場這兩⼤業務的表現：
美國商業客戶營收：達到  9.84 億美元，年增  148%（季增  29% ）。
美國政府客戶營收：達到  9.53 億美元，年增  96% 。

### Page 7

如果把海外市場也加進來，整體商業總營收來到  11.7 億美元，同樣在歷史上⾸
度超越整體政府營收的  11.5 億美元。
⽐起破紀錄的成⻑率，營收組合的翻轉其實更具代表性。
過去市場對  Palantir 的印象，多半停留在政府、國防與情報系統的承包商；但
隨著商業營收全⾯超⾞，華爾街在它⾝上套了⼗年的「政府承包商」標籤，在
這⼀季正式失效。
過去市場對 Palantir 的印象，多半停留在政府、國防與情報系統的承包商。這類
業務雖然⾨檻極⾼，但容易受到政府預算審查與採購週期的影響。
值得注意的是，這次商業營收能夠超⾞，背後依然伴隨著極為強勁的政府業
務。
美國政府營收年增 96% ，幾乎翻倍；純粹是因為美國企業客戶導⼊的速度衝得
更快，讓公司成功⻑出了第⼆顆規模相當、⽽且爆發⼒更強的成⻑引擎。
如果再往下⼀層看，會有⼀個⽐營收交叉更有意思的發現。

### Page 8

第三季美國商業客戶的總數來到 686 家，年增 29% ；但同期美國商業營收卻⼤
增了 148% 。為什麼營收成⻑的速度，會遠遠甩開客戶數量增加的速度？
答案就在於「淨收⼊留存率（ NDR ）」。這⼀季 Palantir 的整體淨收⼊留存率，
從上⼀季的 157% 進⼀步攀升到 165% 。
這說明了推動營收翻倍的主⼒，很⼤部分來⾃既有客戶持續加碼。
就像前⾯提到的製造商⼀樣，⼀家企業可能先⽤ Palantir 管庫存，覺得好⽤之
後，接著把採購、排程、財務到 AI 決策全部放進來。
不過，這波企業 AI 變現潮⽬前還是⾼度集中在美國。

### Page 9

相較於美國商業營收 148% 的⾼成⻑，第三季國際商業營收只有 1.84 億美元，
年增 21% ，季增更只有 1% 。
這意味著美國企業對軟體轉型的接受度依然領先全球，⽽未來能否把美國驗證
成功的模式複製到海外市場，將是後續觀察的重點。
重點三：營收翻倍但員⼯數幾乎沒變 —— ⾼營業槓桿背後的結構
與代價
把前⾯兩件事串起來看：當客戶透過⼯作坊體驗產品後迅速擴⼤採⽤，⽽且導
⼊之後⼜會⾃⼰越買越多（ NDR 165% ），這會對 Palantir 的成本結構帶來什麼
影響？
執⾏⻑ Alex Karp 在接受媒體（ MarketWatch ）訪問時特別提到了⼀個營運細
節：
Palantir 在美國商業營收⼤增 148% 的同時，全公司的員⼯總數（ Headcount ）
幾乎維持持平。

### Page 10

在傳統的企業軟體產業裡，公司如果想把營收規模擴⼤⼀倍，通常得按⽐例招
募⼤量的業務代表、售前顧問與⽀援⼯程師，⼈⼒成本也會跟著⽔漲船⾼。
但 Karp 形容 Palantir 的成⻑基本上不太依賴傳統意義上的業務員。對他們來說，
產品本⾝（ Foundry 、 Ontology 加上 AIP ）在⼯作坊裡的實際表現，就是最好的
銷售⼯具。
更重要的是，因為底層的資料整合與基礎架構在初期導⼊時就已經建好了，當
客戶後續決定把合約從 300 萬美元擴⼤到 1,500 萬美元時， Palantir 並不需要再投
⼊同等⽐例的⼯程⼈⼒從頭開發。
當營收以接近翻倍的速度成⻑，交付這些服務所需的總⼈數卻沒有等⽐例
增加，這展現了軟體業相當強勁的營業槓桿。
這項特質解釋了為什麼 Palantir 能在⾼成⻑的同時，交出亮眼的獲利數字：
第三季 GAAP 營業利益率達到 47% ，調整後（ Non-GAAP ）營業利益率⾼達
62% ，進⽽推升 Rule of 40 來到 158% 的⽔準。
不過，這種⾼營業槓桿背後也有相對應的代價。

雖然公司的總員⼯數幾乎持平，但為了留住頂尖⼈才並獎勵績效，第三季的股
權激勵費⽤（ SBC ）從去年同期的 1.72 億美元⼤幅增加到 3.19 億美元，幾乎翻
了⼀倍。
這也正是  GAAP 營業利益率（ 47% ）與調整後營業利益率（ 62% ）之間相差 15
個百分點的主要原因。
重點四：拆解 TCV 放緩與 RPO 持平的真相

### Page 11

這份完美財報裡唯⼀的裂縫，在於反映未來幾年營收可⾒度的單季「新簽總合
約價值（ TCV ）」與「未履約義務（ RPO ）」出現了明顯放緩：
整體  TCV ：第三季為  29.18 億美元，年增率僅  6% （相⽐第⼆季為年增
49% ），⽽且較上⼀季的  33.73 億美元下滑了  13.5% 。
美國商業  TCV ：第三季為  14.63 億美元，年增率降⾄  11% （相⽐第⼆季為
年增  153% ），為  2023 年以來的最低單季增速。
剩餘交易價值（ RDV ）與未履約義務（ RPO ）：單季來看，整體  RDV 季
增僅  2% （美國商業  RDV 季增  4% ，低於上⼀季的  27% ）， RPO 則幾乎持
平。
當季營收⼤增  96% 、新簽訂單卻只成⻑  6%，這兩個數字在財務邏輯上不可能
⻑期並存。
當季營收主要是由既有客戶擴張所推動，⽽新簽約指標的放緩，⾃然讓部分投
資⼈開始擔⼼未來幾年的營收可⾒度。
針對這個現象，財務⻑ David Glazer 與經營團隊在法說會上的解釋是：

### Page 12

許多客戶為了快速導⼊ Sovereign AI 的初期功能、急著讓系統趕快上線，所以
在第三季簽下了好幾筆千萬美元級別、但「合約期限⽐往常更短」的訂單。
管理層同時強調，「 Sovereign AI ⾰命才剛滿⼀季」，並預期隨著客戶使⽤深
⼊，這些合約的年限與規模都會隨時間增加。
管理層的這番解釋，其實也剛好從會計規則上說明了為什麼 RPO 幾乎持平：因
為 Palantir 的 RPO 定義，本來就不計⼊初始期限少於 12 個⽉的短期合約。
如果第三季新簽的  Sovereign AI ⼤單多半屬於⼀年以內的短約，那麼不會反映
在 RPO 上、同時壓低單季 TCV 總額，確實符合財務邏輯。
此外，如果我們把時間拉⻑來看，⻑約存量指標其實沒有單季數字看起來那麼
疲弱：
整體  RDV （剩餘交易價值）： 年增率仍達  55% 。
美國商業  RDV ： 達到  65.2 億美元，年增率⾼達  79% 。
過去  12 個⽉（ TTM ）美國商業  TCV ： 累計達  61.14 億美元，年增

62% 。
換句話說，單季簽約指標放緩，還不等於企業端的需求轉弱。真正的問題在於
估值給出的容錯空間極⼩。
以約  178 美元的股價與  24.03 億股計算， Palantir 的市值約  4,286 億美元；扣除
帳上  104 億美元的現⾦與美國公債後，企業價值（ EV ）約為  4,182 億美元。
對照全年營收指引中值  85.32 億美元，企業價值倍數（ EV/Sales ）⾼達  49 倍；
就算⽤第四季指引年化的  106 億美元來算，也⾼達約  39 倍。
將近  40 到  50 倍的營收定價，隱含市場要求這家公司在跨過百億美元年化營收
之後，未來⼗年還得維持約  50% 以上的年複合成⻑率。
在這麼⾼的⾨檻下，市場沒有太多耐⼼等待。
下⼀季投資⼈最想驗證的，就是這批短約能不能如管理層所說，順利續約成更
⻑、更⼤的多年期合約。
從更⼤的產業趨勢來看，企業 AI 的投資重⼼正在轉移。
第⼀波浪潮，⼤家搶著買 GPU 、買算⼒、買⼤模型 API ；但進⼊第⼆階段，企
業真正關⼼的是如何結合內部資料與⼯作流程，讓 AI 實際改善營運並省下成

本。
Palantir 已經在美國市場證明⾃⼰能吃到這波轉型的紅利。

### Page 13

接下來決定它⻑期價值的關鍵，就在於這批新導⼊的短期合約能否在未來幾季
轉化為⻑約存量，並將這套商業模式進⼀步複製到更廣⼤的海外市場。
蘋果：營收創紀錄、⽑利被記憶體成本
壓縮， Service 令⼈失望？
Apple 這次財報，表⾯上相當不錯。
第四季營收來到 1,145 億美元，年增 12% ；每股盈餘 2.10 美元，年增 14% 。主
⼒產品 iPhone 營收成⻑ 17% ， Mac 更是⼤幅成⻑ 24% 。

### Page 14

但在這些亮眼數字背後，如果仔細拆解財報細節與管理層在法說會上的說法，
會發現幾個值得留意的變化。
⾸先是⽑利率。
這⼀季表⾯上 47.9% 的⽑利率，其實包含了約 1 個百分點的關稅退款⼀次性收
益（每股盈餘 2.10 美元裡，也有 0.05 美元來⾃同⼀筆退款）。

### Page 15

扣掉這筆⼀次性收益後，本業⽑利率⼤約是 46.9% ；但就算⽤這個基準來⽐，
Apple 給出的下⼀季（ 12 ⽉季度）⽑利率指引落在 45% ⾄ 46% ，依然在往下⾛。
要知道， 12 ⽉季度是 Apple 傳統的銷售最旺季，營收規模放⼤通常會推升⽑利
率，為什麼這次反⽽逆勢下滑？
財務⻑ Kevan Parekh 在法說會上給出了答案：下⼀季⽑利率下滑，「主要」反映
記憶體成本上升，「另外也包括 Siri AI 上線的部分成本」。
這句話其實就是貫穿整份財報的核⼼線索：全球 AI 浪潮正同時從「硬體成
本」與「軟體商業模式」兩端夾擊 Apple 。
AI 確實帶來了新的成⻑機會，但也正在改變 Apple 原本最賺錢的獲利結構。
我們可以把這次財報拆解成五個環環相扣的重點來看：
重點⼀：硬體的成本逆⾵ —— 全球 AI 算⼒戰，為什麼由 Apple
先買單？

### Page 16

下⼀季⽑利率下滑的最⼤推⼿，來⾃記憶體成本的飆升。 這背後反映了⼀個很
現實的產業現象： Apple 雖然想靠 AI 賺錢，但現在卻得先為全球的 AI 資料中⼼
基建買單。
CEO John Ternus 在法說會上直接⽤「前所未⾒（ unprecedented ）」來形容當
前的記憶體價格環境。
其實在 9 ⽉這⼀季， Apple 就已經感受到記憶體漲價的壓⼒，只是當時⼿裡還有
早先囤積的低價庫存，加上其他⾮記憶體零組件成本下降，才抵銷掉部分衝
擊。
但隨著低價庫存逐漸消耗，這股成本壓⼒將在 12 ⽉季度正式浮現；管理層也坦
⾔，在 12 ⽉季度之後，記憶體採購價格預計仍會持續上升，⽽研調機構更普遍
預期這波供應緊俏會⼀路延續到 2027 年。
為了應對這場結構性的缺貨， Apple 已經採取了兩個動作：
直接漲價： 6 ⽉底先調漲了 Mac 與 iPad 的價格， 9 ⽉⼜將 iPhone 售價調⾼
約 100 美元，且管理層並未排除未來進⼀步漲價的可能。

### Page 17

砸錢鎖定產能： Apple 的庫存⾦額單季⼤增 31% 、年增⾼達 154% ，來到約
145 億美元；從 6 ⽉到 9 ⽉季度，製造採購承諾⾦額更是⼀⼝氣暴增了 330
億美元。
憑藉龐⼤的現⾦流與規模， Apple 搶貨的能⼒絕對⽐中⼩型 Android ⼿機廠或 PC
廠強得多。
但記憶體終究是每⼀台硬體裝置中無法妥協的核⼼成本，當漲價轉嫁給消費者
到了⼀定極限、開始有拉⻑換機週期的⾵險時，硬體⽑利率勢必得承受壓⼒。
全球掀起 AI 資料中⼼擴建潮，最先賺到錢的是記憶體⼤廠，⽽包括 Apple
在內的終端硬體商，正在為這場硬體通膨買單。
重點⼆：⾼⽑利新武器卡關 ——iPhone Duo 的產能與初期品管
挑戰

### Page 18

既然記憶體漲價壓縮了硬體⽑利，最⾃然的解法，就是多賣⼀點⾼單價、⾼⽑
利的新產品來拉⾼平均售價（ ASP ）。
這就帶出了市場最關注的新品： Apple ⾸款折疊機 iPhone Duo 。
iPhone Duo 在 9 ⽉底財報結算後才正式上市，因此第四季財報還沒有反映它的
營收。
從需求⾯來看，這款新機⾮常成功，預購迅速秒殺，出貨時間⼀路排到明年⼀
⽉下旬。
但問題出在供給⾯。
這次法說會上， CEO John Ternus 特別安排營運⻑ Sabih Khan ⼀起上線回答供應
鏈問題。
Khan 坦⾔， iPhone Duo ⽬前處於「產能受限」的狀態，且「很難預測供需何
時能達到平衡」。

### Page 19

這種罕⾒不給時間表的說法，意味著折疊螢幕與轉軸的量產爬坡難度，⽐過去
傳統 iPhone ⾼出許多。
更⿇煩的是，初期有極少數⽤戶反映內螢幕轉軸處出現摺痕或裂紋的品質問
題；雖然 Apple 已經在協助換機，但緊繃的產能也讓部分地區的換貨等待時間
拉⻑。
除了折疊機，過去受限於先進製程（隨著產品線轉向 2 奈⽶已逐漸緩解）的
Mac 與 iPad ，到了 12 ⽉季度依然⾯臨缺貨，⽽這次卡住產能的瓶頸，剛好⼜是
前⾯提到的記憶體不⾜。
換句話說，儘管下⼀季營收預計仍能成⻑ 7% ⾄ 10% 、有望創下公司歷史新
⾼，但記憶體漲價加上⾼階新品缺貨，讓硬體業務在短期內很難幫公司撐起⽑
利率。
這時候， Apple 就更需要仰賴⾼⽑利的 Services （服務業務）來穩住獲利基本
盤。
第四季 Services 營收只占公司總營收約 28% ，卻因為⾼達 76% 的⽑利率，貢獻
了全公司將近 44% 的⽑利。

### Page 20

硬體負責把⼈帶進⽣態系， Services 負責賺取⾼利潤，這是 Apple 最核⼼的賺錢
公式。
然⽽，正如財務⻑在⽑利率指引中透露的，連 Services 這座⾼⽑利堡壘，也從
下⼀季開始要⾯對 AI 帶來的成本考驗。
重點三： Siri AI 終於有了收費⽅向，但會不會稀釋 76% 的⾼⽑
利？
Apple 在今年 WWDC 就已經宣布，部分需要雲端模型處理的 AI 功能將設有使
⽤額度，使⽤者未來可以付費取得更多運算容量。
這次法說會上， Goldman Sachs 分析師 Michael Ng 進⼀步追問： iCloud+ 是否正
在轉型成 Apple AI 商業化的重要⼯具？
管理層給出了肯定的答案，確認未來需要更多伺服器端 AI 運算能⼒的使⽤者，
可以付費購買額外容量。
過去⼤家訂閱 iCloud+ ，買的是「儲存空間」，⽤來放照⽚、影⽚和備份檔案。
到了 AI 時代，使⽤者額外付費購買的變成了「運算能⼒」，像是讓 Siri 分析複
雜⽂件、執⾏多次雲端請求，或是呼叫⼤型模型處理⼯作。

### Page 21

坐擁超過 25 億台活躍裝置、超過 15 億個付費訂閱的 Apple ，要推這項新服務當
然很有優勢。
假設未來 Apple 能吸引 5,000 萬⼈、每⽉額外付 10 美元使⽤進階 AI 功能，⼀年
就能帶來 60 億美元的營收，相當於⽬前全年 Services 營收的 4.9% 左右。
但從財務⾓度來看，這⾨⽣意跟過去的數位服務有⼀個本質上的差異。
傳統數位服務的營收增加，不太需要同⽐例增加成本；但⼤型 AI 模型每次執⾏
雲端推論，背後都在消耗真實的算⼒資源。使⽤者越頻繁使⽤複雜的 AI 功能，
Apple 要承擔的伺服器與推論成本就越⾼。

### Page 22

過去每收到 100 美元服務收⼊， Apple 可以留下 76 美元⽑利；但到了 AI 服務
時代，營收增加的同時，背後往往伴隨著持續攀升的雲端推論成本。
這也解釋了為什麼財務⻑ Kevan Parekh 特別強調， Siri AI 的雲端推論費⽤將
計⼊ Services 的銷貨成本，⽽且相關成本已經開始反映在 12 ⽉季度的⽑利率指
引中。
同時， Apple 還透露了⼀個會計處理上的改變：當 Siri AI 成為硬體裝置隨附的服
務時，公司會將⼀部分的硬體收⼊分配給這項服務，並在裝置使⽤期間逐步認
列。
這代表就算消費者沒有額外掏錢加購算⼒， Apple 的 Services 營收與成本結構，
也會因為 AI 功能的推出⽽重新洗牌。
因此，接下來觀察 Apple 的 AI 變現能⼒，除了看有多少⼈願意付費，更要看每
增加⼀美元的 AI 收⼊，究竟能留下多少⽑利。
這也是為什麼 Apple 持續投⼊⾃研晶⽚與裝置端 AI ，除了讓⼿機跑得更順，
背後更有明確的財務考量：把越多的運算留在裝置端處理，⻑期下來就能

省下越多的雲端推論成本。

### Page 23

重點四：華爾街真正擔⼼的事 —— 當 AI Agent 成為⼊⼝，
Apple 還能收多少過路費？
如果說推論成本影響的是「利潤率」，那 Morgan Stanley 和 Evercore 分析師在
法說會上追問的，則是關係到「營收來源」的根本問題：
當 AI 從聊天機器⼈演變成能替使⽤者執⾏任務的 Agent （代理），會不會威脅
到 Apple 現有的 Services 業務？
我們可以想像⼀個很⽇常的場景。
現在如果你想規劃⼀趟旅⾏，通常會先打開 Google 搜尋機票，進到航空公司網
站⽐價，接著打開訂房 App 找飯店，最後分別完成付款。整個過程必須切換好
幾個不同的平台與 App 。
但在不久的將來，你可能只需要對⼿機裡的 AI Agent 說⼀句：「幫我安排下週末
去東京的旅⾏，機票加飯店預算三萬台幣，飯店希望離⾞站近⼀點。」
接下來， AI Agent 就會⾃動幫你搜尋、篩選，並直接串接不同服務完成預訂。
從頭到尾，你可能連訂房 App 都沒有打開過。
這個轉變最核⼼的影響，在於「誰掌握了決定權」。 過去是使⽤者決定要打開

哪個 App ；未來則是 AI Agent 決定要調⽤哪⼀家服務商。

### Page 24

⼀旦消費者習慣把⽇常決策交給 ChatGPT 、 Gemini 或其他第三⽅ AI Agent ，
Apple 過去靠 iOS 與 App Store 築起的收費關卡就會⾯臨兩個挑戰：
App Store 分潤壓⼒： 如果第三⽅  Agent 直接透過網⾴、外部  API 或其他
⽀付管道完成交易， Apple 對數位商品與訂閱交易的抽成能⼒就會被繞
過。
搜尋授權收⼊重分配： Apple 每年從搜尋引擎拿到的巨額授權⾦，建⽴在
「 iPhone 是最重要搜尋⼊⼝」的商業價值上。當  AI Agent 逐漸取代傳統
搜尋與  App 操作，這個⼊⼝的經濟價值勢必會重新洗牌。

### Page 25

⾯對這個問題， John Ternus 的回答展現了 Apple 的防守策略。
他強調， iPhone 是使⽤者最重要的個⼈資訊中⼼，裡⾯存放著聯絡⼈、訊息、
照⽚、⾏事曆、健康與財務資料。
AI 如果想真正理解使⽤者需求並跨 App 完成任務，就必須取得這些個⼈脈絡與
系統權限。
這讓未來的 AI Agent 競爭，分成了兩種不同的控制權：
需求⼊⼝： 誰最懂使⽤者想做什麼，誰來決定推薦哪些商品與服務（這
是  OpenAI 、 Google 正在搶的）。
執⾏⼊⼝： 誰能安全地讀取個⼈資料、呼叫  App 功能，並完成最後的操
作（這是  Apple 透過  iOS 、 App Intents 與  Siri AI 牢牢握在⼿裡的）。

### Page 26

Apple 很清楚⾃⼰未必要做出全世界最龐⼤的模型，但它必須利⽤作業系統與
隱私權限的優勢，把「需求」與「執⾏」整合在⾃⼰的平台上，確保使⽤者依
然透過 Siri 來完成⽇常任務。
⽽這場⼊⼝保衛戰之所以迫在眉睫，從這次 Services 的財測就能看出端倪。
Apple 第四季 Services 營收達到創紀錄的 315.7 億美元，年增約 10% ；但下⼀季
的成⻑指引卻放緩到 8% ⾄ 9% 。
管理層解釋，放緩的主因來⾃「第三⽅授權收⼊」與「匯率」變化，其次則是
部分 App Store 與⼿遊業務的調整。

### Page 27

值得注意的是，管理層⼝中的「第三⽅授權收⼊」，背後很⼤⼀部分就是
Google ⽀付給 Apple 的搜尋引擎分潤。
雖然單季度的波動未必完全來⾃ AI Agent 的衝擊，但這正好印證了華爾街的擔
憂：只要搜尋授權與 App Store 這兩⼤⽀柱稍微放緩， Services 的成⻑動能就會
⽴刻感受到壓⼒。
重點五：現在的股價，到底在賭什麼？
把前⾯硬體與軟體的拼圖全部放在⼀起，我們最後來看估值。
以財報當⽇收盤價約 338 美元、以及 FY2026 實際每股盈餘 8.98 美元計算，
Apple 的歷史本益⽐約為 37.7 倍（財報公布後盤後股價回落⾄約 329 美元，換算
也⾼達 36.6 倍）。

### Page 28

37.7 倍的本益⽐聽起來很貴，但「貴」是⼀個很模糊的詞。換個問法會清楚很
多：如果今天⽤ 338 美元買進 Apple ，然後⼀直抱著不賣，你每年⼤概能賺多
少？
有⼀個很簡單的算法：
⻑期年報酬  ≈ 現⾦殖利率  + ⻑期成⻑率
現⾦殖利率，就是公司⼀年賺進的⾃由現⾦流，除以你付出的市值。
Apple 現在市值接近 5 兆美元，⼀年的⾃由現⾦流⼤約只有市值的 2.6% 。
換句話說，⽤ 338 美元買進，第⼀年真正拿回的現⾦回報只有 2.6% 左右，剩下
的報酬全靠未來成⻑。
⽤這個邏輯反推，可以從兩個⾓度看現在的股價：
如果假設  Apple ⻑期每年成⻑  3% ：
338 美元的股價，換算下來的⻑期年報酬⼤約是  6.1% （註：若以純現⾦殖利率
2.6% 直接相加則約為  5.6% ）。

### Page 29

同⼀時間，美國⼗年期公債殖利率約  4.9% ，⽽且幾乎沒有⾵險。
這代表買  Apple ⽐買公債多承擔了股價波動的⾵險，每年卻只多換來約  1 個百
分點左右的⾵險補償；⽽在歷史上，美股投資⼈要求的⾵險補償通常落在  4%
到  5% 。
如果投資⼈要求每年  7% 的報酬：
這只⽐公債多  2 個百分點左右，已經算很客氣了。
但要做到這樣， Apple 必須永遠每年成⻑約  3.6% 以上。
⼀般估值模型給成熟公司的永續成⻑率，多半抓在  2% 到  3% ；要求  3.6% ，等
於要求⼀家市值近  5 兆美元的巨頭，永遠跟著整個美國經濟的速度⼀起⻑⼤。
這兩個⾓度講的是同⼀件事：現在的股價，幾乎沒有留空間給意外。
這時候再回頭看⽑利率，就能理解市場為什麼對那 1 到 2 個百分點的變化這麼
敏感。

### Page 30

以 2026 財年全年 Services ⾼達 1,233 億美元的營收規模來算，服務⽑利率只要
每下降 1 個百分點，⼀年就少掉約 12.3 億美元的⽑利。
若放⼤到全公司約 4,790 億美元的總營收來看，全公司⽑利率每少 1 個百分點，
扣稅後⼤約會少掉每股 0.27 美元的盈餘；⽤ 37.7 倍的本益⽐換算，等於股價直
接少了約 10 美元。
更別忘了，連計算 37.7 倍本益⽐所⽤的 8.98 美元 EPS 起點，裡⾯都還包含了⼀
筆關稅退款的⼀次性收益。
所以這次財報最值得思考的地⽅，在於現在的股價，其實已經先預設了 Apple
會有個完美結局：
市場預設記憶體漲價很快會過去、 Siri AI 的雲端算⼒成本控制得住，⽽且第三
⽅ AI Agent 也搶不⾛ iPhone 的流量⼊⼝。
對 Apple 來說，投⼊ AI 同時是⼀項成⻑投資，也是⼀筆為了保護每年 1,233 億
美元服務⽣意的必要⽀出。
只要硬體缺料漲價、 AI 推論成本攀升、或是⼊⼝分流這三件事當中有任何⼀件

拖得⽐預期更久，市場除了要下修獲利預估，連那 37.7 倍的估值倍數本⾝都可
能⾯臨重新定價。
⽽在現在這個價位，後者帶來的影響往往更⼤。

### Page 3

3
Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

4
Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015 -2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd

~£136mn of direct costs related to the

also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.

What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.

What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product
specifications and pricing), following expiry
of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating

operational disruption. Underperformance

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.

dbDataInsights Consumer Survey Methodology
—Our proprietary survey conducted in 2026 (January – September) asked

consumers how they would respond if a company they shopped at regularly
was involved in a data breach or cyberattack, and how they would respond
once the disruption was resolved. This monthly survey covers households and
consumers in Germany, France, Italy, Spain, the United Kingdom, and the
United States.
—For each country referenced above, a nationally representative sample of more
than 500 households is surveyed each month (over 600 households in the US).

Collectively, this meant over the course of the survey (January – September
2026), we surveyed almost 30,000 households.

### Page 18

18
Material cyber incidents have contributed to negative credit outlooks
The infrastructure exists for cyberattacks to have a greater impact on credit quality should cyber disruption continue to accelerate
Source: S&P, Moody’s, Fitch, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Credit rating agencies consider both pre-breach resilience frameworks and
post-breach financial impacts. Agencies assess pre-breach cyber preparedness
through a lens of mitigating risk, while post-breach credit impacts centre on

financial capacity to withstand the resulting shock (considers scale and length of
the disruption); highly leveraged issuers with limited liquidity are seen as more
vulnerable. Negative outlook changes at JLR and Equifax illustrate the potential
credit impact. Fitch generally considers permanent reductions in cash generation
more credit-significant than temporary cash-flow delays.
—Ratings agencies are moving towards continuous monitoring. In 2021,

Moody’s invested $250mn in BitSight, which produces daily security ratings.
Moody’s has since built BitSight’s ratings into its credit tools, including Credit
Catalyst and EDF-X, for uses such as underwriting and ongoing monitoring of
borrowers and suppliers. This means lenders can see a company’s exposed
vulnerabilities as a potential indicator of corporate cyber risk.
S&P Global:
“The increasing frequency of cyberattacks and the potential for rapid deterioration in credit

profiles after an attack are risk factors relevant for our rating assessments.”
Moody’s:
“Cyberattacks can undermine the creditworthiness of companies, organizations and
governments, across all sectors and geographies, hitting revenues, increasing costs, and eroding
customer trust.”
Fitch Ratings:
“Although cyber events cannot be predicted with great accuracy, the associated risk can be
managed through proactive strategies, technology, policies, and vigilance.”

“The chief executive officer and board of directors hold ultimate responsibility for managing
cyber risk.”
Example rating actions where cyber was a named factor

Cyber governance & breaches can impact ESG scores
Sectors that hold the most personal data, and monetise it most directly, carry the greatest risk weighting in mainstream ESG scores
Source: MSCI ESG Ratings Methodology, Morningstar Sustainalytics ESG Risk Ratings, SASB Materiality Map, Deutsche Bank Resear ch.
Cybersecurity is generally seen as both a social and governance consideration
MSCI and Morningstar Sustainalytics both assess data privacy and security through management of the

risk, exposure to it and the controversies that follow a breach. Weak governance weighs on the score,
and a breach can cut it directly – more severe controversies draw larger deductions that persist for
longer.
Materiality varies sharply by sector. SASB flags data security as material across most consumer,
financial, healthcare and technology industries, with customer privacy concentrated in the most data-
intensive ones (see right).

### Page 20

IV. Investment, risk and opportunity

### Page 21

21
Europe’s dependency on US tech is firmly in the spotlight
Access to frontier AI models is now a security question; the funding gap for Europe will be hard to close
Source: Tikehau Capital – Tikehau Capital Barometer of European Investment in Cybersecurity – 7th edition 2026, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Growing cyber risk creates increased dependency on US cyber tech. Growing
concern over the cyber capabilities of frontier AI models has made ensuring

access to such technology a national security priority. US dominance over the AI
tech chain (e.g. chips, model development, etc), its willingness to impose AI-
related controls / restrictions, and previous pressure against Europe over tech
regulations has amplified concerns that dependence on US tech represents a
strategic threat to European sovereignty.
—Funding is the structural challenge. US cybersecurity start-ups attract almost 6x
the funding of European peers, and US-domiciled firms dominate the major

indices – 89% of the Nasdaq CTA Cybersecurity index and 93% of Global X
Cybersecurity ETF (which seeks to track the performance of Indxx Cybersecurity
Index) by weight. Europe has also seen home-grown firms such as Darktrace
acquired by US companies, though this reflects broader market trends rather
than anything cyber-specific. Ultimately, the US has a greater exit strategy for
start-ups as well.
—Boosting homegrown solutions a focus, but also a risk. EU policymakers are

seeking to address the dependency; e.g. the EU’s Cybersecurity and AI Action Plan
includes “scaling up Europe’s AI capabilities for cybersecurity” as one of its three
objectives. However, there are also risks associated with this endeavour; as one
dbDataInsights survey participant put it, “for most European organisations, the
bigger near-term danger would be moving too fast to less capable providers.”
—The wider relationship adds to the exposure. A more transactional

international environment, alongside Europe’s dependency on the US for energy
supplies and defence support, compounds the risk. Recent US-EU diesel
negotiations serve as a pertinent example of this, whereby the US reportedly
considered a diesel export ban to compel a release of European diesel reserves.

### Page 22

22
Cybersecurity as an opportunity
Tailwinds include demand related to keeping operations secure with AI acceleration and consolidation tailwinds
Source: dbDataInsights, Deutsche Bank Research. Investors, defined as those who invest in stocks/ shares (either individually or through a broker/advisor) on a regular basis , were asked whether they perceived cybersecurity / data privacy as a risk or an opportunity for investors in 2026.
Broadly, DB’s company research view is that cybersecurity becomes even

more important in the AI era. Whereas cybersecurity has historically been viewed
as a blocker/drag on innovation and operational speed, it is now viewed as a
strategic enabler for secure AI adoption.
In particular, network security may see renewed growth after being viewed as a
more mature category. Rising AI usage should drive additional compute and data-
centre capacity, expanding the infrastructure requiring segmentation and
protection. Agentic workloads may also generate lower-throughput but more

frequent and persistent interactions across models, tools and data sources,
increasing the volume and complexity of network-level inspection.
Vendor consolidation has been a structural trend in cyber for some time, though not
necessarily linear. That said, the current environment may accelerate the
consolidation tailwinds as customers look to close security gaps that may arise from
a fragmented tool stack.
See further commentary from DB’s company research team here.

Our March 2026 “personal investor” survey showed a more positive view
among “ESG” investors. 58% in the US and 52% in the UK saw cybersecurity/data
privacy as an opportunity. Among all investors, US respondents favoured
opportunity over risk (46% vs 30%). UK views were more balanced (38% vs 40%).

### Page 24

24
Appendix A - Attack types
Top Attack types What it is How AI is helping attackers
Malware Software built to damage, disrupt or gain unauthorised access AI writes harmful software faster. Google has found malware that rewrites its own code while running to avoid detection, and criminal marketplaces selling AI malware-building tools.
Phishing Deceptive messages that trick people into giving up sensitive information Where AI’s effect is clearest: fluent, personalised lures without the old spelling and phrasing giveaways. Google says state-backed groups use AI to mass-produce them, and AI can fake voices and video.

Denial of service (DoS) Flooding a system so it cannot serve legitimate users Little reported evidence so far. AI can help find weak points and automate traffic floods, but the attack itself stays fairly simple.
Distributed denial of service
(DDoS)
The same attack launched from many
compromised devices at once
AI can accelerate the development and automation of attack tooling, while large-scale
botnets provide the distributed capacity behind hyper-volumetric DDoS attacks.

Man-in-the-middle (MitM) Intercepting or altering traffic between two parties Mainly indirect. AI-written phishing pages and deepfake voices make it easier to trick people into connecting through an attacker or approving fake sign-ins.
SQL injection Malicious SQL queries injected to manipulate backend database operations AI tools scan sites and spot flaws in database code faster, so less-skilled attackers can find weaknesses that once needed real expertise.

Cross-site scripting (XSS) Scripts injected into web pages to hijack user sessions As with SQL injection: AI speeds up finding vulnerable pages and writing the malicious code.
Zero-day exploits Attacking a software flaw before a patch is released A major new development. In May 2026 Google reported the first zero-day it believes was developed with AI. Its September 2026 report says the bigger effect is attackers misusing known flaws faster, before fixes land.

Advanced persistent threats
(APTs)
An intruder staying hidden in a network for long-
term
Google reports state-backed groups using AI across the whole attack, from target
research and lures to tooling and sifting stolen data.
Ransomware Encrypting data or locking device and demanding payment to release it NCSC expects AI to add to the global ransomware threat by lowering the skill needed to break in. AI also helps criminals find the most extortable data.

IoT exploitation Abusing weakly secured connected devices for entry AI can scan the internet for unpatched cameras, routers and storage devices and identify which flaws they carry.
Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, National Cyber Security Centre, Google Threat Intelligence Group, Cloudflare, FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3)

Appendix B - European vs US cyber regulation
Europe US Federal
Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026
Regulation Key requirements
NIS2 Directive In force 2023, transposition deadline Oct-24. Covers 18
sectors (up from seven) and creates an EU vulnerability
database. In-scope firms must manage cyber and
supply-chain risk and notify authorities of significant
incidents. Management bodies must approve and
oversee cyber risk measures, with potential liability for

infringements.
Cybersecurity Act In force 2019. Establishes an EU-wide certification
framework for ICT products, services, processes and
managed security services, and made the EU
cybersecurity body ENISA permanent.
Cyber Resilience Act In force 2024, main obligations from 2027.
Manufacturers and retailers of products with digital
elements must deliver security by design, a defined
support period and security fixes within it, plus
mandatory vulnerability management and disclosure.

Digital Operational
Resilience Act (DORA)
In force 2023, applies from 2025. Financial-services
specific, covering own-firm and third-party risk. Requires
an ICT business continuity policy, regular resilience
testing and disclosure of major incidents, and gives
regulators oversight of critical ICT providers.
Cyber Solidarity Act In force 2025. Creates a European Cybersecurity Alert
System to detect threats and share cross-border
warnings, plus a Cyber Emergency Mechanism to boost

preparedness and response.
Regulation Key requirements
White House Accord on
Super Intelligence
(2026)
Emphasises self-regulation to ensure AI safety, with a call for
external evaluations and the possibility of formal regulation in
future. Not legally binding; signed by President Trump and
leading AI companies.
Executive Order on
Cybersecurity (2025)
Highlights risk from foreign actors and orders further action
on defending digital infrastructure, securing the most vital

and governance.
CIRCIA (passed 2022,
not yet in effect)
In-scope entities, focused on critical infrastructure, must
report covered incidents to CISA within 72 hours and
ransomware payments within 24 hours of payment.
Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act
(amended 2024)
Financial institutions must explain information-sharing
practices and safeguard customer data, running a security
programme covering risk assessment, service-provider
oversight and incident response.

Federal Information
Security Modernization
Act (2014)
Applies to federal agencies and contractors: system security
plans, continuous monitoring, incident response capability
and mandatory incident reporting.
Federal Acquisition
Regulation (cyber
provisions 2016)
Governs federal procurement, setting baseline cyber
requirements for suppliers so cyber risk is built into
government contracting. Agencies may impose stricter
provisions.

Document 526: ステーブルコインは「国債の買い手」になるのか ～デジタルドルが米国債を支える構図は、日本でも生まれるのか～

Source PDF: all report pdf/ステーブルコインは「国債の買い手」になるのか ～デジタルドルが米国債を支える構図は、日本でも生まれるのか～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-02
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

Research Report

2026 年 11 月 2 日

1/6 
ステーブルコインは「国債の買い手」になるのか 
～デジタルドルが米国債を支える構図は、日本でも生まれるのか～ 
 資産運用調査部 主席研究員  柏村 祐

（要旨） 
○ 米国の巨額の財政赤字や伝統的な買い手の保有縮小により、米国債の「需給懸念」と金利高
止まりが意識される中、暗号資産市場から生まれた「ステーブルコイン」の発行体が新たな
買い手として浮上している。 
○ ステーブルコイン発行体による米国債保有は急増しており、中国の保有減少を一部補う規模
に達している。また、IMF の実証分析でも、ステーブルコインへの需要増加が短期米国債利
回りに低下圧力を及ぼすことが示されている。 
○ 一方、法定通貨担保型ステーブルコインの99.4％は米ドル連動であり、円建ての市場規模は
極めて小さい。当面、円建てステーブルコインが日本国債の需給を支える存在になることは
期待しにくい。 
○ 個人投資家は、米金利を占う上でステーブルコイン市場の動向を新たな金融市場の指標とし
て注視しつつ、国内資産については従来型の金利上昇（債券安）リスクへの備えを継続する
ことが対応策の一つとなる。

1. 米国債の「需給懸念」と金利高止まり～新たな買い手は誰か～ 
米国の金利動向は、世界の金融市場や個人投資家のポートフォリオに多大な影響を及ぼす。足
元では、インフレの高止まりや米連邦準備制度理事会（FRB）の金融政策動向に加え、米国債の
「需給懸念」が長期金利の上昇（債券価格の下落）を招く構造的な要因の一つとして意識されて
いる。米政府の財政赤字が拡大を続ける一方で、米国債の主要な海外保有国の一つである中国
は、外貨準備の多様化などを背景に米国債の保有を減らしつつある。こうした伝統的な買い手の
一部が保有を減らす中、 「一体誰が今後の米国債を買い支えるのか」という問いは、米国株のバリ
ュエーションやドル円相場の行方を左右する論点である。 
こうした中、米国債の新たな買い手として急速に存在感を高めているのが、暗号資産市場から
生まれた「ステーブルコイン」である。法定通貨に価値を連動させる代表的なステーブルコイン
は、その価値を裏付けるために巨額の準備資産を必要とする。 
ステーブルコインは米国債の有力な買い手となり、米国債の利回り上昇を抑える要因になり得

### Page 2

2/6 
るのか。また、デジタルドルが米国債を支えるこの構図は、日本国債と円建てステーブルコイン
の間でも起こり得るのだろうか。本稿では、最近の実証研究を手がかりにこの資金の流れを検証
し、個人投資家にとっての論点を整理する。

2. ステーブルコインは国債の新たな買い手となるか 
(1) 米国債の新たな買い手は誰か 
近年、暗号資産市場の拡大とともに、法定通貨（主に米ドル）に価値を連動させる「ステーブ
ルコイン」の発行残高が拡大している。その発行体は、発行したコインの価値を裏付けるため、
準備資産として安全かつ流動性の高い資産を保有する必要がある。その代表格が短期米国債（米
国財務省短期証券、T-bills）である。 
サンフランシスコ連銀が2026年 9 月に公表したレポートによれば、ステーブルコイン発行体に
よる米国債の保有が急増しており、米国債市場における新たな買い手として浮上している。過去
20年間、米国債の外国人保有比率は低下傾向にあり、特に中国などの外国政府による保有減少が
目立っていた。しかし、ステーブルコイン発行体は過去5年間で米国債関連資産の保有額を約
2,000 億ドル増加させており、これは同時期の中国による米国債保有減少額の4 割超に相当する。
ただし、中国の保有減少は主に長期国債であるのに対し、ステーブルコイン発行体の保有増加は
主に短期米国債であり、満期構成には違いがある。 
資料 1は、中国とステーブルコイン発行体（時価総額上位2銘柄であるUSDTおよびUSDCの発行
体（それぞれTetherおよびCircle））の米国債保有額の推移を示している。足元のトレンドが継続
した場合、ステーブルコイン発行体による短期米国債の需要は、2030年末までに約 4,000 億ドル
へとほぼ倍増するとの試算も示されている。伝統的な買い手が後退するなか、短期ゾーンに限っ
ていえば、ステーブルコイン発行体はすでに主要な買い手の一角を占めつつある。

### Page 3

3/6

資料 1 ステーブルコイン発行体の米国債保有額は急増し、中国の保有減少を一部補う 
（注）ステーブルコイン発行体は時価総額上位 2 社（Tether（USDT）および Circle（USDC））を指す。保有額には米国財務省短期 
証券（T-bills）および現先取引（レポ）を含む。 
（出所）Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, “Stablecoin Issuers’ Growing Appetite for Treasury Securities”をもとに第一ラ 
イフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(2) ステーブルコインは国債金利を押し下げるのか 
ステーブルコインによる米国債需要の増加は、実際の金利形成にどのような影響を及ぼすのだ
ろうか。国際通貨基金（IMF）が 2026年3 月に発表したワーキングペーパーは、ステーブルコイ
ンの需要増加が金融市場に与える影響を実証的に分析している。同ペーパーでは、マクロ経済全
体の動き（好景気や株高など）による影響をできるだけ切り分けるため、ステーブルコイン特有
のニュース（規制動向や技術提携など）を手掛かりに、需要の変動を識別する手法（ナラティブ
識別）を用いている。 
資料 2は、この手法を用いて「ステーブルコインの時価総額をピーク時に1％増加させる需要シ
ョック」が生じた際、1カ月物米国債利回りが時間の経過とともにどう反応するか（インパルス応
答）を推計したものである。これを見ると、ショック発生後に利回りは持続的に低下し、約24 週
後に最大で約1.9ベーシスポイント（bp）の押し下げ効果が生じると推計されている。押し下げ
幅そのものは小さいが、ステーブルコインへの需要が国債市場の需給を通じて、短期金利に波及
する経路が実証的に示された意義は小さくない。暗号資産市場の動きが、もはや同市場内の出来

### Page 4

4/6 
事にとどまらなくなっていることを示している。

資料 2 ステーブルコインへの需要増加は短期米国債利回りの低下要因となる 
（注）ステーブルコイン（USDT および USDC）の合計時価総額をピーク時に 1％増加させる需要ショックに対する、1 カ月物米国債 
利回りの推定反応（インパルス応答）。「ナラティブ識別」とは、ニュースなどの定性的な情報をもとに、他の経済要因の影響
を受けにくい出来事を選び、特定のショックを識別する分析手法。青色の実線は推定値、濃い青色と薄い水色の網掛け部分
はそれぞれ 68％および 90％の信頼区間を示す。 
（出所）IMF, “Stablecoin Shocks” (WP/2026/044)をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

(3) 円建てステーブルコインは日本国債の買い手になるか 
米国市場で起きている「デジタルドル（米ドル建てステーブルコイン）が米国債を支える」と
いう構図は、日本でも起きるのだろうか。日本においても、法整備が進み円建てステーブルコイ
ンの発行・流通が本格化すれば、その準備資産として日本国債（特に短期国債）への新たな需要
を生み出す余地はある。 
しかし、現時点では米ドル建てと円建ての市場規模には圧倒的な差が存在する。国際決済銀行
（BIS）が2026年 6 月に公表した「Annual Economic Report 2026」によれば、法定通貨担保型ス
テーブルコインの99.4％が米ドルに連動している。資料3 は、米ドル建て以外のステーブルコイ
ンの時価総額を米ドル建てに対する比率で示したものだが、ユーロ建てやブラジルレアル建てな
どと並び、円建て（JPY）の規模は米ドル建ての0.01％台に過ぎず、極めて小さい水準にとどまっ
ている。 
円建てステーブルコインが日本国債の有力な買い手として市場に影響を与えるようになるに
は、決済や価値保存の手段として円建てステーブルコインのユースケースがグローバルに拡大
し、時価総額が飛躍的に成長することが前提となる。円建ての規模が米ドル建ての1万分の1 程
度にとどまる現状を踏まえれば、当面、円建てステーブルコインが日本国債の需給を左右する局

### Page 5

5/6 
面は想定しにくい。日本の投資家にとっての論点は、円建てではなく、米国市場ですでに起きて
いる変化の側にある。

資料 3 米ドル建て以外のステーブルコインの市場規模は依然として極めて小さい 
（注）米ドル建てステーブルコインの時価総額に対する各通貨建てステーブルコインの時価総額の比率（％）。2026 年 5 月 29 日 
時点。法定通貨担保型ステーブルコインのみを対象とする。 
（出所）BIS, “Annual Economic Report 2026”をもとに第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

3. 個人投資家へのインプリケーション～「暗号資産×マクロ」の新視点～ 
前節で見てきたように、ステーブルコインの発行体は米国債市場で存在感を高めており、その
需要拡大が短期金利に低下圧力を及ぼすことも実証研究によって示されている。一方で、円建て
ステーブルコインの市場規模は極めて小さく、日本国債の需給に影響を及ぼす存在になるには、
なお市場の大幅な拡大を要する。 
個人投資家にとっての含意は2 点ある。 
第一に、米国の金利動向を占う上で、「ステーブルコイン市場の動向」を新たな金融市場の指標
として注視すべきであるということだ。これまで、暗号資産は伝統的な株式や債券とは異なる投
機的な投資対象と見なされがちだった。しかし、ステーブルコインを介して暗号資産市場の資金
が国債市場に流れ込み、金利形成に影響を与え始めている。暗号資産を保有していない投資家で
あっても、ステーブルコインの時価総額の伸びや、米国で成立したGENIUS法の 2027年 1月の施
行や関連規制の動向が、自身の保有する米国株や米国債券ファンドの価格に影響を及ぼす可能性
がある点に留意が必要だ。 
第二に、日本国内の金利上昇リスクに対する警戒を怠らないことである。米国ではステーブル
コインという「新たな買い手」が国債需給を下支えする構造が生まれつつあるが、日本では当面
同様の需給下支え効果は期待しにくい。日本銀行（日銀）が国債買入れの減額を進め、長年市場
を支えてきた最大の買い手が後退する中、日本国債についても、需給の緩みを通じた金利上昇圧
力には注意が必要である。

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
個人投資家にとっては、ステーブルコインの拡大による米国短期国債の利回り低下を、一つの
市場シナリオとして念頭に置きつつも、国内資産については、保有する債券のデュレーション
（金利変動に対する価格の感応度）を短めに保つなど、従来型の金利上昇（債券安）への備えを
継続することが、対応策の一つとなる。

成長率目標の達成が射程圏内に
◆７～９月期のGDPは小幅持ち直し
７～９月期の実質 GDP成長率は 、前年比＋
4.5％と、前期から伸び率が拡大 。１～９月累計
では同＋4.6％と、中国政府が掲げる2026年の成
長率目標の範囲内 。好調な外需が景気を押し上
げ。輸出入額には原油や半導体などの価格上昇の
影響も大きいものの 、実質ベースでは輸入よりも輸
出が伸びており 、GDP成長率に対する純輸出の寄
与度が拡大。
一方、内需は引き続き低迷。とりわけ投資が弱く、
総資本形成のうち固定資産投資は前年比マイナス
幅が拡大。インフラ投資などで持ち直しの動きが見ら
れるものの、過剰生産や不動産不況といった構造的
な下押し要因が残存。
GDPデフレーターは前年比＋ 1.4％と、２期連続
のプラス。もっとも、第二次産業でのエネルギー・原材
料高の価格転嫁が主因であり、内需拡大によるイン
フレとは言い難い状況。
◆成長率目標は達成見込み
10～12月期の成長率は７～９月期を若干上

回り、2026年の成長率目標は達成される見通し。
好調な外需に加え 、追加の内需刺激策が景気を
下支え。「６つのネットワーク」やハイテク製造業といっ
た重点分野向けの金融緩和策が９月末に打ち出
されたほか、住宅ローンの利子補給制度が10月から
開始。もっとも、内需の本格回復には至らず 、景気
の基調としては回復力が弱い状況が続く見通し。

消費は総じて低調
◆サービス消費は堅調も、財消費が低迷
７～９月期の小売売上高は前年比＋0.7％と、
弱い動き。財消費の低迷が主因であり、なかでも自
動車が大幅減。買い替え補助政策で喚起された需
要の反動減に加え 、原油高でガソリン車需要が減
退。宝飾品も、国際的な金価格の上昇と節約志向
の高まりが相まって、販売数量が大幅減。
先行きの消費も、総じて低調に推移する見通し。
中東危機によるガソリン価格の高止まりなどを背景に、
家計の購買意欲は低迷 。政府は利子補給制度の
対象拡大などで内需を喚起しているものの、大規模
な消費刺激策には慎重な姿勢。一方、サービス消
費は今後の消費拡大の重点分野に位置づけられて
おり、堅調に推移する見込み。
◆住宅市場は長期停滞から脱却できず
住宅市場は引き続き低迷。大都市を中心に住宅
購入要件の緩和などが実施されているものの 、購入
層の減少といった構造的な要因が住宅販売の重石
に。新築住宅価格に加え 、市場の実勢を反映しや

すい中古住宅価格も引き続き下落。在庫の回転期
間も長期化している模様。
こうしたなか、政府は住宅ローンの利子補給制度
を初めて導入 。地方での需要喚起が見込まれるも
のの、実施期間が１年限定であることから政策効果
は一時的にとどまる見通し 。住宅需要の構造的な
弱さも残るため、住宅市場の本格回復にはつながら
ない見込み。

◆投資は下げ止まり
９月の固定資産投資は前年比 ▲7.2％と、２ヶ
月連続でマイナス幅が縮小 。セクター別にみると 、イ
ンフラ投資が底打ち 。 「６つのネットワーク 」（六張
網）の推進により、情報伝送や航空輸送に関連す
る固定資産投資が足元で増加 。製造業も、ハイテ
ク産業の輸出増加がけん引役となってマイナス幅が
縮小。一方、住宅市場の低迷を背景に、不動産開
発投資は大幅減少が継続。
◆年末にかけてマイナス幅が縮小
先行きも、六張網によるインフラ投資の押し上げや、
ハイテク産業での投資拡大が続く見通し 。両者に関
して、政府は金融緩和策を発表。中国人民銀行か
ら政策銀行への貸出である担保付補完貸出
（PSL）の金利を引き下げるとともに 、PSLの支援
対象に六張網を追加 。また、科学技術イノベーショ
ンと技術改造（生産技術の高度化 ）向けの再貸
出限度額を拡大。
もっとも、本格回復は見込めない状況 。製造業や
不動産業では、過剰な生産能力や在庫などの構造

的な下押し要因が残存。製造業の設備稼働率は、
春節の反動で第２四半期に上昇する傾向があるも
のの、本年は第２四半期以降も続落 。ハイテク製
造業などの一部産業を除き、多くの産業で稼働率が
低下。インフラ投資も量的拡大は限定的。財源とな
る地方債は借り換えや隠れ債務の圧縮が優先され 、
重い債務負担が地方政府による投資拡大の制約。

外需は先行きも堅調
◆外需が頼みの綱も、燻るリスク
実質GDP成長率に対する純輸出の寄与度は拡
大。名目輸出額はAIブームを背景とする半導体な
どの価格上昇で大幅に増加したほか 、７～９月期
は実質ベースでも輸出が輸入を上回る伸び 。米中
関係の改善を受け、対米輸出は前年比プラスで推
移。また、中東危機を契機に世界的な脱炭素化が
一段と加速しており 、EV輸出が拡大。ASEANなど
への中間財輸出も好調を維持しており、先行きも総
じて堅調に推移する見通し。
一方、貿易不均衡の拡大がリスク 。10月、EUが
中国からのハイブリッド車（HV・PHV）に輸入制限
を課す方向で双方合意 。加えて、中国政府は新エ
ネルギー車 （NEV）輸出増値税の還付率を引き
下げ、海外での低価格競争の抑制を図る方針を表
明。こうした動きは摩擦緩和に資するも 、EU側には
さらなる規制強化を求める声 。輸出増値税の変更
を前にした駆け込み輸出がEUや他国の警戒感を煽

る恐れも。輸入規制が幅広い品目や地域に広がれ
ば、外需依存を強める中国経済に打撃。
◆資金流出は拡大傾向
金融収支は流出傾向が継続 。対内直接投資が
減少しているほか、準備資産も縮小。商業銀行など
への外貨資金の振替・預託や資産再配分が進行し
ている可能性。先行きも、内需の低迷が対内投資
の重石となり、金融収支でみた資本流出傾向が継
続する見通し。

◆根強いディスインフレ圧力
９月の生産者物価（PPI）は前年比＋5.0％と、
伸びが加速 。原油価格に起因する生産財価格の
再上昇が主因。一方、消費者物価（CPI）は、総
合ベースで同＋0.9％、コアベースで同＋1.0％と、
横ばい圏で推移 。交通燃料のプラス幅が再び拡大
したほか、半導体の価格高騰で通信機器が上昇し
ているものの 、インフレ率は政府目標の２ ％前後を
大きく下回っており 、内需の停滞に起因するディスイ
ンフレ圧力が強い状況。
先行きも、エネルギー価格の高止まりや半導体価
格の上昇を背景に一部財の価格上昇圧力は根強
いものの 、内需の低迷により基調的なインフレ率は
低位で推移する見込み。
◆対米ドルレートは増価基調
10月の人民元の対米ドルレートは増価基調で推
移。中東危機が経済に与える影響や金融政策の
動向を見極める動き市場で続くものの 、貿易不均
衡の是正や資本流出の抑制に向けた人民銀行の
元高容認姿勢もあり、小幅な人民元高圧力が続く

見通し。
◆株価は下落
10月の上海総合株価指数は下落。原材料価格
の上昇率が一服したことを好感して 持ち直す動きが
みられるものの 、中東情勢の不透明感や内需の弱
さが相場の重石に。景気の停滞が続くなか、先行き
も弱い動きが続く見込み。
インフレ率は政府目標に届かず

日本総合研究所主催 シンポジウムのご案内
米中対立の常態化や米国の自国第一主義の強まりを背景に、既存の国際経済秩序が揺らいでいます。こうしたなか、各国は存
在感を高めるグローバルサウス諸国との外交・経済関係の強化を急いでいます。日本も、OECD（経済協力開発機構）などと協調し
ながら、関係強化を目指していますが、現状はASEANやインドなどに偏重しており、アフリカや中南米との連携は限定的です。本シン
ポジウムでは、はじめに世界経済の構造変化や日本のグローバルサウス諸国との連携策の現状・課題を多角的に検証します。その
上で、日本がグローバルサウス諸国との関係を深化させるための具体的な戦略を議論します。
